<meta>This page continues the critique of failed alchemists began on the previous page. While page 49 described the physical toll—bleary eyes and sulfurous smells—this page focuses on the intellectual and practical errors of the "Sophists" (charlatans) who use the wrong materials and mock those who actually succeed. Institutional stamps from the Biblioteca Apostolica Vaticana are noted in the left margin.</meta>

<margin>Stamps of the Vatican Apostolic Library</margin>

And so you may see the **Sophists** <term>Sophistas|In this context, "Sophists" refers to false alchemists or charlatans who use deceptive or "clever" logic rather than following the true laws of Nature.</term> suffer many hardships,
  Whom their own error keeps wandering in misery;
And when every resource has been consumed by vain labor,
  They hurl frequent taunts <note>original: "scommata." These are biting, sarcastic jests or insults directed at those who are more successful.</note> at the true **Wise Men** <note>original: "Sophos." The poet uses this Greek-derived term to distinguish the true adepts from the "Sophists."</note>.
They wished to be called "Philosophers" everywhere; but they
  Lack all knowledge of the **secret stone** <note>original: "Arcani lapidis." Another name for the Philosopher's Stone, the legendary substance capable of transmuting base metals into gold.</note>.
This one boils **urine**; another seeks the "seed" in **soot**;
  Another calls for the "first seeds" to come down from the stars.
This one takes his material from **eggs**; that one takes **blood**;
  This one foolishly divides the first **Elements** <term>Elementa|The four fundamental qualities (Earth, Air, Fire, Water) that alchemists believed composed all matter.</term> of the matter.
He multiplies the shards of shattered pots, and breaks very many 
  Vessels, but he remains utterly far from the right path.

<summary>This page satirizes the "Sophists" or false alchemists who waste their lives and resources on absurd ingredients like urine, soot, and blood. It contrasts these "vessel-breakers" with the true "Sophos" (Wise Men) who possess the actual secret of the Philosopher's Stone.</summary>

<meta>This page continues the satirical catalog of the "Sophists'" failures, moving from their physical toll to a mockery of their cluttered laboratories and their nonsensical use of alchemical jargon. It follows the previous page's description of their wasted resources and "biting jests" against true masters.</meta>

It is a truly laughable thing to survey the houses of these men;
  Look at the **furnaces** and the **vessels** you see there!
What **strong waters** <term>aquas fortes|Corrosive acids, such as nitric acid, which were used by alchemists to dissolve metals.</term> you perceive there! What salts and liquids?
  And among the powders, **sulfur** and **arsenic**?
How well those talkative men know how to chatter about the 
  **First Matter** <term>Materia Prima|The formless, original substance that alchemists believed was the necessary starting point for the Great Work.</term>, which they have never known, much less seen!
They praise "our" **Mercury**; and as for "our" **Sulfur**,
  The more they speak of it, the more foolish they become. <note>The author uses "our" (nostrum) to mock how charlatans adopt the terminology of true adepts to sound legitimate.</note>
**Red wine** <note>original: "Vinum rubrum." An allegorical term for the purified, white mercurial substance required for the "White Work" or silver-making.</note> are always on their lips;
  And they spout endless **discourses** <note>original: "logos." The poet uses the Greek word for "words" or "reasoning" to highlight the pretentiousness of the charlatans.</note> about the **fifth essence** <term>quinto ente|The Quintessence or Aether, the purest element that was thought to permeate all things and provide the "soul" of the Philosopher's Stone.</term>.
They speak of **honey**, **afterbirths** <note>original: "secundinas." This refers to the placenta; the poet lists it to highlight the bizarre and often repulsive materials that "Sophists" experimented with in their confusion.</note>, and a thousand other names—
  Terms that are often astonishing just by the sound of them.

<summary>This page satirizes the cluttered laboratories and pretentious jargon of false alchemists, who hide their ignorance behind complex terms like "Quintessence" and "White Wife" while working with useless or disgusting materials.</summary>

->They have earned the name of **Multipliers** <term>Multiplicatores|A derogatory term for fraudulent alchemists who claimed they could "multiply" a small amount of gold into a larger mass.</term>, and to be called<-

--- Page 113 ---
<meta>This page continues the polemic against fraudulent alchemists (the "Multipliers") mentioned on the previous page. Having established their lack of knowledge and their deceitful nature, the text now turns to the legal and financial consequences of their actions, describing their interactions with victims (goldsmiths and merchants) and their eventual imprisonment.</meta>

<margin>SP <note>Likely an abbreviation for "Sententia Poetae" (The Poet's Opinion) or "Sub Poena" (Under Penalty), marking a transition to the legal consequences of fraud.</note></margin>

Listen to the **goldsmiths** <term>Aurifabros|Craftsmen who worked with gold; they were often the first to identify debased or counterfeit metals produced by "multipliers."</term>, and the complaints of the **merchants**;
Consult the **officers of the law** <term>lictores|Originally Roman civil servants who attended magistrates, here used to refer to bailiffs or those tasked with arresting criminals.</term>, so that you may be certain of the truth.
Truly, one shameful group gapes longingly at **lost coins** <note>original: "amissis nummis." Refers to the capital wasted on failed alchemical experiments.</note>,
While another spends even more, chasing after **empty profits**.
This wretch begs for himself a single **pound** <note>original: "libram." A unit of weight or currency.</note> out of ten,
And while he squanders his own wealth, he is forever in want.
Meanwhile, the **officer** keeps those he has seized in **prison**;
When he opens their purses, he finds only **false seals** <note>original: "falsa sigilla." These could be forged hallmarks on metal bars or fraudulent legal documents used to trick investors.</note>.
There is as much **money** to be found here as there is **water** in a **pumice stone** <note>original: "quantum sub pumice limpha." A classical Latin idiom meaning "absolutely nothing," as a pumice stone is porous and dry.</note>;
To such a degree are they miserable, and everywhere found **guilty**.
Hence a "happy" **prison** is their due, so that there
They may enjoy a welcome **leisure** in secure **quiet** <note>The author concludes with biting sarcasm, suggesting that the only "rest" these frantic fraudsters will find is behind bars.</note>.

If he has less knowledge of the **Noble Arts of Phoebus** <note>original: "ingenuas Phoebi... artes." Phoebus Apollo was the god of both healing and hidden knowledge; in alchemy, his "arts" refer to the enlightenment and technical skill required for the Great Work.</note>,

If the work succeeds, he will happily repay you for everything,
->So that you may receive the worthy rewards of your merit.<-

<margin>57</margin>
You yourself will pay heavy penalties in the Tartarus of Dis <note>original: "Ditis Tartara." And why you must shun the company of the wicked.

For as a victor you hold the palm of triumph from the **Fifth**.

--- Page 124 ---
<meta>This page transitions from the Fifth Gate (Putrefaction) to the Sixth Gate, "Congelation." It continues the instructional poem, moving from the ethical warnings of the previous section into the specific chemical/symbolic requirements for solidifying the alchemical matter.</meta>

# VI

# ON CONGELATION <term>Congelation|Original: "congelatio." This is the sixth stage or "gate" of the alchemical process. It is the process of thickening or solidifying a liquid substance, often referred to as "fixation," where volatile "spirits" are made stable and resistant to heat.</term>

In what manner our **material** <note>original: "materies." The raw substance of the Great Work.</note>, once condensed, may be frozen,
There is no need for me to say much; I shall sing of a few things.
It has the power to make liquid things harden into a **white color** <note>original: "albo colore." This signifies the stage of *Albedo* (the White Work), where the matter is purified of its blackness and begins to shine with a snowy brilliance, indicating it is nearing the state of the White Stone.</note>,
And to halt the retreat and the flight of the **spirits** <note>original: "spirituum... fugam." In alchemy, "spirits" are volatile substances like mercury or sulfur that vaporize easily. "Halting their flight" means the alchemist has successfully "fixed" them so they stay in the vessel even under high heat.</note>.
If ever you freeze our **Stones** into ash,
Then I wish for you to be less anxious.
For you will see the **Elements** suddenly converted into themselves,
If they have been **putrefied** <note>original: "putrefacta." Referring to the previous stage of decay (Nigredo) which is necessary for the subsequent rebirth and solidification of the matter.</note> in a natural way.
But the method of the foolish in the art of freezing is different,
And thus, in vain, he dissolves bodies into clear water:

<note>Vocabulary: congelation, material, spirit, stones, elements, putrefaction, alchemy</note>

<summary>This page introduces the Sixth Gate of the alchemical process, Congelation, explaining how the previously liquid and volatile "spirits" are fixed into a solid, white state. It emphasizes that this solidification can only happen naturally if the previous stage of Putrefaction was performed correctly.</summary>
<keywords>Congelation, Sixth Gate, Albedo, Fixation, Volatile Spirits, Elements, Putrefaction</keywords>

--- Page 125 ---
<meta>This page continues the discourse on the Sixth Gate, "Congelation," providing a critique of the "fools" who attempt the process through literal, physical means rather than the secret philosophical method. It maintains continuity from the previous page's warning about those who dissolve bodies in vain.</meta>

He washes the **salts** <note>original: "sales." In alchemy, salts represent the solid, fixed principle or the "body" of the substance being worked upon.</note>, and again twice and thrice he dissolves them,
Then freezes these into a flowing matter.
Regarding these things, the **fools** <note>original: "stulti." A common term in hermetic texts for "puffers" or literal-minded chemists who do not understand the spiritual or symbolic nature of the art.</note> chatter much nonsense among themselves,
While they ponder the foolish work of their hands.
For they dissolve their **elements** <note>original: "Elementa." The fundamental components of the matter (earth, air, fire, water) which must be harmonized.</note> with their hands, 
Finally freezing them into the form of a dry powder.
But that method of freezing is not what we seek,
For it is utterly unsuited to my **Work** <note>original: "operi." The *Magnum Opus* or Great Work of alchemy.</note>.
For the secret freezing of our **Stone** <note>original: "Lapis." The Philosopher's Stone.</note> never, 
At any time, fears the force or the threats of **Vulcan** <note>original: "Vulcani." The Roman god of fire; used here to represent the intense heat of the alchemical furnace. A "congealed" stone is "fixed" and will not vaporize.</note>.
Indeed, this **fatty substance** <note>original: "pinguis." Often used to describe the "unctuous moisture" or "philosophical sulfur" that allows the Stone to fuse with and transform other metals.</note> remains always in the fire,
And ever abounds through the riches of the **Tincture** <note>original: "Tincturae." The transformative power of the Stone to "dye" or transmute base metals into gold.</note>.

<summary>This page distinguishes between the "foolish" chemical method of freezing salts into powder and the "philosophical" congelation, which creates a Stone that is fire-resistant and holds the power of transmutation.</summary>
<keywords>Congelation, Salts, Vulcan, Philosopher's Stone, Tincture, Elements, Fixation, Alchemical Fire</keywords>

--- Page 126 ---
<meta>This page continues the discourse on the Sixth Gate, "Congelation." It provides a specific critique of the physical properties of the "Stone" during this stage, distinguishing the "true congelation" from the false results produced by "Sophists." It flows from the previous page's discussion of the "fatty substance" and the "Tincture" by explaining how the matter must behave when exposed to heat.</meta>

When the **Water** <note>original: "Aqua." In alchemy, this refers to the 'mercurial' or solvent principle of the matter.</note> is sought, that which is congealed does not melt
&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;Into a thin liquid, for in that way the **Work** <note>original: "opus." The *Magnum Opus* or Great Work of alchemy.</note> would be destroyed.
Therefore it is not frozen in the manner of a hard stone,
&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;Nor is it like crystal, or the hardness of glass.
It is not something that the violence of a flame melts; rather, it is that
&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;Which flows when melted in the manner of **wax** <note>original: "cerae." This quality of "fusibility" is essential; the Stone must be able to melt and flow to penetrate and transform base metals.</note>.
So beware, for that is not the true congelation of the **Stone** <note>original: "Lapidis."</note>,
&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;Which returns quickly into liquids, in the manner of **salt** <note>original: "salis." Common salts dissolve easily in water, but the alchemical salt must be "fixed" and resistant to such dissolution.</note>.
But rather that which flows entirely: which remains constant in the **fire** <note>original: "igne."</note>,
&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;And retains its powers without being changed.
For such a congealed mass of salts is of no use;
&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;For it is in this common way that the **Sophist** <note>original: "Sophista." A term for a false alchemist who works with literal, common substances rather than the "philosophical" principles.</note> congeals.

<summary>This page describes the physical requirements of alchemical Congelation, explaining that the true Stone should melt like wax and remain stable in fire, rather than dissolving like common salt or hardening like glass.</summary>
<keywords>Congelation, Sixth Gate, Philosopher's Stone, Wax-like flow, Fixed Salt, Alchemical Fire, Sophist, Fusibility</keywords>

--- Page 127 ---
<meta>This page continues the technical instructions regarding the "Sixth Gate" of alchemy, Congelation. Having established in the previous page that the "Stone" must not be hard like common glass but fusible like wax, the author now provides the specific procedural steps: the necessity of putrefaction, the purification of elements, and the reunion of body and spirit once the matter reaches the stage of "whiteness."</meta>

If you desire, therefore, to proceed by the right path: never
&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;Let the **Medicine** <note>original: "Medicina." This refers to the Elixir or the Philosopher's Stone, which was believed to "heal" base metals into gold and restore human health.</note> flow by anything other than natural force.
Nor can it come to rest when bound by its own liquids,
&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;Unless it has first been **putrefied** <note>original: "putrefacta." Putrefaction is the essential stage of decay and blackening—the *nigredo*—where the old forms are broken down so a new substance can be born.</note> under the guidance of **Nature**.
First, you must purify our **Elements** <note>original: "Elementa." In this context, it refers to the basic alchemical components of the work—often Earth, Air, Fire, and Water—which must be cleansed of impurities.</note>, and in this way let them be congealed
&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;While joined together, and soon let them flow as soft as **wax**.
For when the **matter** <note>original: "materies."</note> has been made perfectly **white** <note>original: "candida." This marks the *albedo* stage, the second major phase of the Great Work, signifying purity and the preparation for the final "red" stage.</note>,
&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;The **bodies** must be reunited with their own **spirits** <note>original: "spiritibus." Alchemists viewed the "spirit" as the volatile, vaporous part of a substance and the "body" as the solid residue; the two must be "married" to achieve stability.</note>.
A long period of time must be established for this stage,
&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;Until you see these congealed substances appearing like **gems** <note>original: "gemmas." This refers to the crystalline, sparkling appearance of the fixed Stone, indicating its high degree of perfection.</note>.
When you behold these things, take the joy to yourself
&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;With a happy countenance, and reverently give thanks to God.

<summary>This page outlines the transition from the purification of alchemical elements to the "Albedo" or whitening stage, emphasizing that the "Medicine" must first undergo putrefaction before it can be fixed into a jewel-like state. It concludes with an exhortation to the alchemist to remain patient and grateful to the Divine upon reaching this milestone.</summary>

<meta>This page continues the alchemical poem, moving from the process of "Congelation" into the specific sequence of color changes—the "Four Pillars" of the Great Work: black, white, and red. ->Afterwards, the grains will bring back to you **red blood**,<- <note>original: "rubeum... cruorem." This represents the *rubedo* or "reddening," the final stage of the alchemical process signifying perfection and the creation of the Red Stone or Elixir.</note>

->Indeed, no one has denied these first principles of things:<-
->This has been the single voice of all the **Philosophers**.<- <note>original: "Philosophis." Here meaning the "Adepts" or true alchemists of history, such as Hermes Trismegistus.</note>
->If **blackness** presents itself to you in dark garments,<- <note>original: "nigredo pullis se vestibus offert." The "dark garments" or funeral clothes symbolize the heavy, opaque, and "dead" state of the matter.</note>
->There will also be a certain hope of a **glowing color**.<- <note>original: "candentis... coloris." This refers to the *albedo* or "whitening," the stage of purification that follows the blackness.</note>
->Once they have been bound together into a **whitening color**,<-
->Then you possess a **Stone** of no small value.<- <note>original: "Lapidem." The Philosopher's Stone. At the white stage, the Stone was believed to be able to transmute base metals into silver.</note>
->And just as the dry at last becomes **rotten** through the moist,<-
->The **black color** appears from there first of all.<-

<meta>This page continues the alchemical poem from page 59. While the previous page focused on the "death" of the material in the black stage (nigredo), this page describes the transition into the white stage (albedo) through the action of heat and the "fixing" of the substance.</meta>

->Thus indeed, if **moisture** is congealed by dry elements,<- <note>original: "humiditas quod si per sicca geletur." In alchemical theory, the "congelation" of a liquid into a solid represents a stabilization of the volatile spirit.</note>
->**Whiteness** shines at night like a clear light.<- <note>original: "Albedo noctu lux quasi clara micat." The *albedo* is the second major stage of the Great Work, symbolizing purification and the "white stone" which can transmute base metals into silver.</note>
->And thus they proceed little by little, and they whiten the whole<-
->**Matter**, so that it surpasses even flakes of snow.<- <note>original: "Materiam... floccos vincat ut illa nivis." Snow is a common metaphor for the extreme purity of the philosophical white stage.</note>
->**Moisture** reveals itself from the black, and from there<-
->It brings various forms of colors, and new ones among the various.<- <note>original: "Humiditas nigro se prodit... colorum fert varias formas." This refers to the "Peacock’s Tail" (*cauda pavonis*), a brief phase where many colors appear before the matter settles into pure white.</note>
->The cause of these was always the **active heat**, and the same heat<- <note>original: "calor agens." This is the "fire of the philosophers," a steady, controlled heat that matures the substance in the flask.</note>
->Which continually moves and nourishes the **seeds**.<- <note>original: "Semina." Alchemists believed that matter contained "seeds" or life-principles that would "grow" into gold or the Elixir when properly incubated.</note>
->By this reasoning, in its own members, both inside and out,<-
->The **matter** stands varied in its appearance:<- <note>original: "Materies vultu stat variata suo."</note>
->Not as fools play under a false image,<- <note>original: "falsa ludunt sub imagine stulti." A reference to "puffers" or fraudulent alchemists who used chemical tricks to give metals a superficial coating of silver or gold.</note>
->Which, since it is less **fixed**, cannot withstand the **fire**.<- <note>original: "fixa... igne." To "fix" a substance means to make it non-volatile, so that it does not evaporate or change when exposed to the intense heat of a furnace.</note>

<summary>This page describes the transition from the black stage of alchemy to the white stage (albedo), emphasizing that steady heat matures the "seeds" within the matter to create a stable, purified substance that can withstand fire.</summary>

<keywords>albedo, whiteness, active heat, seeds of matter, fixation, cauda pavonis, purification, alchemical fire</keywords>

--- Page 130 ---
<meta>This page continues the alchemical poem from page 60. Having established the white stage (albedo), the text now describes the final maturation of the "Stone" through the analogy of biological digestion, moving toward the red stage (rubedo).</meta>

->But in such a way that every part may endure the whole fire,<-
->A stable **Tincture**, and also easily melted in its own way.<- <note>original: "Tinctura stabilis, fluxibilisq." A <term>Tincture</term> is the concentrated essence of the Stone. "Fluxible" means it has become like wax, melting easily without evaporating, which proves it is "fixed" and stable.</note>

->Such a **digestion** occurs within the **stomach**, even before<-
->The white mass emerges from its own **chyle**,<- <note>original: "E chylo veniat candida massa." In medieval medicine, <term>chyle</term> was a milky fluid produced in the <term>stomach</term> during the first stage of **digestion**. Alchemists used this as a metaphor for the early, white stage of their Work.</note>

->And just as heat completes and drives another **digestion**,<-
->Which is the cause of the red color within the **liver**:<- <note>original: "iecori complet agitq. calor." Ancient physiology taught that the <term>liver</term> used internal **heat** to "cook" white chyle into red blood. The alchemist mimics this by "cooking" the white matter until it turns red.</note>

->In no other way does this **Stone**, by the power of dry **heat**,<-
->Glow white; and at its height, it turns red throughout its parts.<- <note>original: "Lapis hic... rubet." This describes the transition to the *rubedo*, the final "red" stage of the Great Work, signifying the completion of the Philosopher's **Stone**.</note>

->But it is helpful here to know another secret: in what way<-
->Is the **offspring** born here in the upper regions?<- <note>original: "fetus / Nascitur... superis... plagis." The <term>offspring</term> refers to the maturing Stone. The "upper regions" refer to the neck or top of the glass vessel where vapors condense and "reborn" substances appear.</note>

->Do not strive too much in blowing upon the **coals**: with these things<-
->I do not deceive you, nor do I fashion a joke for you.<- <note>original: "flandis carbonibus." This is a warning against "puffers"—false alchemists who used heavy bellows and high heat. True alchemy requires a gentle, steady heat, often compared to a brooding hen.</note>

<summary>This page explains the maturation of the alchemical substance by comparing it to human digestion, where white fluid is transformed into red blood. It marks the transition from the white stage (albedo) to the final red stage (rubedo) and warns the reader against using excessive, violent heat.</summary>

<keywords>albedo, rubedo, digestion, Tincture, physiological analogy, Philosopher's Stone, heat control, puffers</keywords>

<meta>This page continues the alchemical instruction from page 60. While the previous page discussed the biological analogy of "digestion" within the "liver" and "stomach" to reach the red stage, this page answers how the "offspring" (the Stone) is animated by explaining the role of the universal Soul or Spirit that mediates between the physical and spiritual realms.</meta>

->But believe me, all your **labor** will vanish into the breezes,<- <note>original: "labor." The author warns that physical work alone is useless without understanding the spiritual principles.</note>
->And the expense spent on the work will be in vain,<-
->Unless your **earth** is made alive through its own **waves**,<- <note>original: "terra... undas." In alchemy, <term>Earth</term> refers to the solid, heavy matter of the work, which must be "vivified" or animated by a liquid "wave" or solvent to progress.</note>
->Our frozen **matter** never joins together.<- <note>original: "Materies... gelata coit." Matter that is "frozen" or "congealed" is spiritually dead; it requires the addition of "soul" to become active and unify.</note>
->There is a **soul** standing between the **earth** and the **sky**,<- <note>original: "anima distans inter terramq, polumq." The <term>Soul</term> (Anima) is viewed as a bridge between the physical world and the heavens (the pole).</note>
->Rising from the earth, like a light breeze from the sea;<-
->This gives life to all things created in the world,<-
->It warms our **Elements** with its own movements.<- <note>original: "Elementa fovet." The four elements (Earth, Air, Fire, Water) are nurtured and balanced by the movement of this universal spirit.</note>
->This soul is airy: a secret and innermost **fire**;<- <note>original: "secretús et intimús ignis." This is the <term>Secret Fire</term>, a non-physical heat that drives the chemical and spiritual transformation of the Stone.</note>
->This is called **Oil**, and the **mystic water** by the **Sages**.<- <note>original: "Oleum, et mystica limpha Sophis." The <term>Sages</term> (Sophis) are the master alchemists of old. They used many names for the volatile spirit, including "oil" because of its richness and "water" because of its fluidity.</note>
->This is the middle air; which is called our **fire**;<-
->This is the oil, the ointment: the **spirit**; the wave: the **Stone**.<- <note>original: "Lapis." The Philosopher's **Stone** is ultimately revealed to be the same substance as the animating spirit, once it has been fixed into a physical form.</note>

<summary>This page describes the "Soul" (Anima) or "Secret Fire" that acts as a mediating force between heaven and earth to animate the "frozen" matter of the alchemical work. It identifies this spirit as the true essence of the Philosopher's Stone, using various technical aliases like "mystic water" and "oil."</summary>

<keywords>Soul, Anima Mundi, Secret Fire, Elements, Philosopher's Stone, Sages, Vivification, Oil, Spirit</keywords>

<meta>This page continues the alchemical instructions from page 61, moving from the definition of the universal Soul to the specific conditions required for the "seat of wisdom" within the vessel. It emphasizes that the work is a self-contained process of "water moving water," excluding external interference like common fire.</meta>

->**Wisdom** establishes her seat in this thing alone,<- <note>original: "sapientia." In alchemical literature, Wisdom is often personified as a divine guide or the "Sophia" who reveals the secrets of the Stone.</note>
->This matter always possesses this place as its own.<-
->**Air** does not exit alone, nor does it enter alone:<- <note>original: "aer." This refers to the circulation of gases or "spirits" within the hermetically sealed vessel; nothing is allowed to escape or be added from the outside.</note>
->**Fire** will confer no help to this work.<- <note>original: "ignis." The author likely refers to "vulgar" or common fire. Alchemists believed that intense, direct heat would destroy the "vegetable" life of the Stone, requiring instead a gentle, "secret" internal heat.</note>
->Then **water** immediately releases it from the doors, and leads it inside,<-
->Waters are never separated from **waters**.<- <note>original: "aquis nunquam dissociantur aquae." This is a fundamental alchemical maxim: "nature rejoices in nature." It suggests that the spiritual and physical liquids of the work are of the same essence and must remain unified.</note>
->And thus a **wave** alone is able to move this **wave**:<- <note>original: "unda." The "wave" represents the <term>Philosophical Mercury</term>, the liquid solvent that acts as the primary agent of change.</note>
->This movement is the cause of **life**, and at the same time, of **death**.<- <note>original: "vitae... necis." A reference to the cycle of <term>putrefaction</term> (death of the old form) and <term>vivification</term> (birth of the new, perfected form).</note>
->The **liquid** adheres to its own **liquids** by the gift of **Nature**,<- <note>original: "Limpha." Referring to the clear, pure moisture or "water of life" extracted during the process.</note>
->And thus nothing resists, nothing stirs up fierce wars.<- <note>This implies a state of perfect chemical and spiritual harmony where the elements no longer struggle against one another.</note>
->This possesses the **soul** of the one leading it out, and the vigorous nature of **lime**:<- <note>original: "anima... calcisque." <term>Lime</term> (Calx) is the white powder remaining after calcination. Here, the "vigorous nature" suggests the dry, fiery principle of the earth has been successfully joined with the "soul" or spirit.</note>
->Therefore it is found less by the foolish.<-

<summary>This page explains that the alchemical transformation is a self-contained process driven by internal "waters" and "waves" rather than external fire. It highlights the harmony of the elements and the necessity of a "soul" to animate the "lime" or physical matter of the Stone.</summary>

<meta>This page continues the allegorical description of the alchemical process, shifting from the "breaking of the waters" to the specific internal dynamics of the vessel. It employs the common alchemical motif of the "Chemical Marriage" between siblings to explain how the substances interact through heat to reach perfection.</meta>

The **work of nature** for the wise man is the **joining of things**;
This occurs just as a **womb** is wont to foster the **seed**. <note>original: "matrix sperma fovere." In alchemical practice, the "womb" or *matrix* refers to the glass flask (the cucurbit or retort), and the "seed" refers to the <term>Prima Materia</term>, the raw substance being transformed.</note>
For just as **sister** and **brother** are the seed, and **heat**
Acts here, while the other is acted upon: he is the **brother**, she the **sister**. <note>The brother and sister represent the active (masculine/Sulfur) and passive (feminine/Mercury) principles of the work. Their union is a central theme in hermetic philosophy.</note>
These two are turned into **one** by the guidance of **Nature**,
So that from here the **perfect work** may be completed.
The first and last part of the **body** is the same, until
It passes through to be perfected into a **rightful man**. <note>original: "iustum... virum." This suggests that the final "Stone" is a perfected, "resurrected" version of the initial material.</note>
A **single thing** takes other forms upon itself, but from the outside
One species does not act suitably upon another species. <note>The author emphasizes that the transformation must be internal and "of one kind." You cannot create gold by adding unrelated materials; the potential must exist within the original substance.</note>
Thus your **generative material** always prepares forms
For itself in a **single vessel**, entirely through **heat**. <note>original: "materies genitalis." The essential substance that has the power to "procreate" or multiply into the final Stone.</note>

<meta>Catchword: It is for you (Est tibi)</meta>

<gloss>Nature, seed/sperm, womb/matrix, work, generative material, heat, brother, sister, union</gloss>

<summary>This page describes the alchemical process as a biological union within a "womb" (the vessel), where masculine and feminine principles (the brother and sister) are fused by heat into a single, perfected body. It emphasizes that the transformation must be self-contained and natural, rather than forced by external substances.</summary>

<meta>This page continues the instructional poem on the alchemical process. Having previously established the internal dynamics of the "Chemical Marriage" and the necessity of keeping the work within "one species," the author now offers three analogies from the natural world—animal, vegetable, and mineral—to justify the hermetic method of incubation within a single sealed vessel.</meta>

And so that you do not lack **examples from nature**, observe
In what way a **chick** is created from an **egg**. <note>original: "Ex ovo pullús." The egg is a primary alchemical symbol for the sealed vessel—often called the *ovum philosophicum* or "Philosophical Egg"—where the "chick" or the Stone is nurtured by constant, gentle external heat.</note>
A **transformation** occurs within the shell many times, until
A **similar species** is born, through the power of **heat**.

Nor do examples of **plants** lack for the seeker;
From any of its own **seeds**, a plant grows green,
When **moisture** acts, mediated by **heat**: nor without
These **principles**, as it were, can a plant grow. <note>The author argues that growth is a universal law. Just as a seed requires moisture and warmth to sprout, the alchemical "seed" (the base matter) requires the <term>radical moisture</term> and a controlled furnace to "vegetate" or evolve.</note>

In the same way, the **radical moisture** also nourishes **minerals**,
Since that moisture was, of course, their very **origin**. <note>original: "radicalis... humor." This refers to the fundamental, life-giving fluid believed to exist within all matter. In minerals, it was thought to be a metallic vapor that condensed into ore within the earth.</note>
They do not depart from their **own kind**; they are born in **one**
**Vessel**, to be perfected through **countless stages**. <note>original: "per innumeros... gradus." This refers to the "degrees of fire" or the successive chemical changes (color shifts) that the matter must undergo within the glass flask before reaching the final state of the Philosopher's Stone.</note>

<summary>This page uses analogies of a chick developing in an egg and a plant growing from a seed to explain how minerals are perfected within a sealed alchemical vessel. It emphasizes that heat and "radical moisture" are the universal engines of growth across all kingdoms of nature.</summary>
<keywords>generation, philosophical egg, radical moisture, mineral growth, heat, vessel, alchemy of nature</keywords>

<meta>This page continues the instructional poem on the alchemical process. While the previous page used analogies of eggs, plants, and minerals to explain the necessity of a single vessel and internal moisture, this page moves into the specific laboratory practice of dissolution and the union of the two primary principles—the "male" and "female"—within the "mother water."</meta>

There, we transform that **one thing** into various forms,
Specifically when it is carried into the **mother water**. <note>original: "matrem... aquam." In alchemy, the "mother water" refers to the initial solvent or the "Philosophical Mercury" which dissolves the solid matter to begin the process of rebirth.</note>
Without this principle, all labor will vanish into **thin air**,
And you prepare your **wood and hearth** in vain.
For the whole work is nothing other than **dissolved seed**, <note>original: "sperma solutum." The "seed" of metals was believed to be a concentrated essence that needed to be "dissolved" or liquefied to release its generative power.</note>
Where **species** joins with **species**, forming a pair in number.
And that which always **acts** is called by the name of the **male**,
While that which **submits** <note>original: "quod patiatur." Literally "that which suffers" or is the passive recipient of action. In the <term>Great Work</term>, the "male" (Sulfur) is the active, shaping force, and the "female" (Mercury) is the passive, receptive matter.</note> bears a **feminine** name.
May she gently hold these most excellent things for the **mother**,
And cherish the **joined seeds** within her breast.
For let these be turned and rotated, until **nature** creates
**Fixed bodies** for herself from these **reborn waters**. <note>original: "limphis." Referring to the purified alchemical liquids. "Fixed bodies" (original: "corpora fixa") are substances that have become stable and resistant to the transformative power of fire, a key goal in creating the Philosopher's Stone.</note>

<summary>This page describes the dissolution of the "one thing" into a "mother water," where the active male and passive female principles (Sulfur and Mercury) unite. This union and subsequent circulation are intended to transform volatile liquids into a "fixed" or stable alchemical body.</summary>
<keywords>mother water, dissolved seed, male and female principles, fixed bodies, Sulfur and Mercury, dissolution, Great Work</keywords>

<meta>This page continues the instructional poem from page 63, moving from the general principle of joining "male" and "female" seeds to the specific nourishment of the metallic seed within a solvent. It focuses on the "menstruum" or solvent required for the growth of the alchemical Work.</meta>

Thus, in its own specific form, the **seed of the metal** is transformed
->So that it may be able to multiply its own **species**.<- <note>original: "sperma metalli... speciem... multiplicare." In alchemy, the "seed" of a metal was believed to contain the blueprint for its growth; "multiplying the species" refers to the goal of increasing the quantity and quality of the gold or silver produced.</note>
While things are done here by **nature** in this order, the **seed**
->Grows, and it brings **nourishment** to itself.<-

In the alchemical laboratory, a <term>menstruum</term> is a solvent—a liquid that dissolves a solid—derived from the Latin word for "monthly," relating to the natural cycles of growth and the preparation of the "womb" for the seed.</note> and this liquid
->Draws its power from the **earth, green in color**.<-
And while **Phoebus** hides his light at this very time, <note>original: "Phoebus." A poetic name for the Sun, representing the active, "hot" principle of Gold or Sulfur.</note>

The "course through the North" or the "midnight sun" refers to the <term>nigredo</term> or blackening stage, where the matter appears dead or hidden in darkness before its eventual purification and rebirth.</note>
This **menstruum** is that blood of our **Green Lion**, <note>original: "viridis cruor ille Leonis." The <term>Green Lion</term> is a famous alchemical symbol usually representing a powerful acidic solvent or a specific raw mineral state. It is the "blood" or life-force that dissolves the metallic seed.</note>
->And not of **vitriol** (take heed of what is told to you).<- <note>The author provides a common alchemical warning: do not confuse the "Green Lion" of the philosophers with common vitriol (sulfuric acid/iron sulfate), which was often seen as too corrosive or "dead" for the Work.</note>
Gracious **Venus** will be the first able to tell you the truth, <note>original: "Alma venus." Representing the planet/metal Copper, but also the principle of attraction and harmony that allows the different elements to bond.</note>
->If only you call her into your counsel with an **open mind**.<-

<summary>This page describes the "feeding" of the metallic seed using a specific solvent known as the "Green Lion's blood," which allows the metal to multiply its own form. It uses the allegory of the Sun (Phoebus) traveling through the darkness of the North to represent the hidden, internal stage of the alchemical transformation.</summary>
<keywords>metallic seed, Green Lion, menstruum, Phoebus, Venus, alchemical solvent, multiplication, nigredo</keywords>

<meta>This page continues the alchemical poem regarding the preparation of the "menstruum" or solvent. It moves from the identification of the Green Lion's blood to the process of "concoction" (ripening through heat) and the eventual coagulation of the seed into a white, solid state known as the "crown."</meta>

These secrets remain silent, though it is through the experience of such things
->That one is granted a name among the **Philosophers**.<- <note>In alchemy, a "Philosopher" is not merely a thinker but a successful practitioner who has mastered the secrets of nature.</note>
This extracted **blood** comes forth from the **heart of the Lion**,
->Though it is known to be not yet sufficiently **concocted**.<- <note>original: "cor Leonis... concoctus." Following the previous page's mention of the "Green Lion," its "blood" refers to a potent acidic solvent. <term>Concoction</term> is the alchemical process of ripening or "cooking" a substance with gentle heat to bring it to maturity.</note>
For here the **proper heat** is lacking; and this very blood
->Is commonly called our **secret menstruum**.<- <note>original: "menstruum." A technical term for a solvent that can dissolve solids, likened to the biological cycles that sustain life.</note>
Consider also that this **seed** of ours is properly nourished,
->When it passes into the **dregs of the body**.<- <note>original: "feces corporis." This refers to the solid impurities or the "earth" that remains at the bottom of the vessel during the process of putrefaction.</note>

VII

## ON FEEDING. <note>original: "De Cibatione." <term>Cibation</term> is a technical alchemical term for the process of "feeding" the dried matter by gradually adding liquid—often called "mercurial milk"—to restore its moisture and volume.</note>

Now I shall speak of the true method of feeding:
To this subject, the seventh place was deservedly assigned.
This nourishes the **dry ashes** <note>original: "cineres." These are the calcined, powdery remains of the matter after it has been purified by intense heat.</note> well with **milk and food**;
Let both be administered only with a frugal hand,
Until, through this diligent study, the third order returns to you:
But lest you **suffocate** the work, you shall give these things quite sparingly.
Beware lest the **belly** <note>The "belly" (original: "venter") refers to the central, rounded part of the alchemical flask or "philosopher's egg" where the transformation occurs.</note> swells from an overflow of water,
Lest a flood bring harmful destruction upon you.
Therefore, administer only a small and modest amount of food and drink,
And take only as much food as will be beneficial.

<note>The vocabulary notes in the original—cibatio (feeding), ratio cibandi (method of feeding), cineres (ashes), lacte (milk), diluvium (flood), and ministra (administer)—emphasize the biological metaphor of the "Stone" as a living thing that requires careful nursing.</note>

<summary>This page describes the seventh stage of the Great Work, "Cibation," using the metaphor of feeding a child. The alchemist is cautioned to nourish the "dry ashes" of the matter with liquid very sparingly to avoid "drowning" or suffocating the delicate chemical reaction.</summary>

<header>VIII</header>

# ON SUBLIMATION

Now it is pleasing to describe the **eighth gate** in a few words,
    Which carries the **body** <term>body</term> (original: "corpus"): In alchemy, this refers to the solid, physical matter or the base metal being transformed. to the heights through a sublime step.
Indeed, fools lift their own "bodies" into the heights,
    Whose foolishness you must not imitate.
But you shall sublimate in this way: if you turn the body into thin air,
    And if, from that air, a body is returned to you. <note>This describes the cycle of evaporation and condensation. The alchemist "spiritualizes" the solid matter into vapor and then "re-incorporealizes" it into a purified solid.</note>
Certain fools dissolve **Mercury** <term>Mercury</term> (original: "Mercurium"): The fluid, volatile principle of alchemy, often associated with the soul or the transformative power of the Work. with **vitriol** <term>vitriol</term> (original: "Vitriolo"): A group of sulfate minerals (like green or blue vitriol) used to create strong acids. and salts:
    They believe they are able to **fix** <note>original: "figere." To "fix" a substance is to make a volatile or liquid material solid and stable so that it no longer evaporates when heated.</note> it in this way.
There are those for whom the **spirit** <term>spirit</term> (original: "Spiritus"): The volatile, gaseous essence of a substance. is extracted
    From scales of iron and tiny bits of steel, and is carried to the heights of the heavens. <note>original: "ad alta poli," literally "to the heights of the pole," referring to the celestial pole or the highest point of the sky.</note>

<summary>This page describes the Eighth Gate of alchemy, Sublimation, warning the reader against the false methods of "fools" who use corrosive salts and vitriol. It defines true sublimation as the process of turning a solid body into vapor and then back into a purified solid.</summary>
<keywords>Sublimation, Eighth Gate, Mercury, Vitriol, Fixation, Spirit, Alchemy, Purification</keywords>

--- Page 147 ---
<meta>This page continues the discussion of the Eighth Gate, Sublimation. The author is contrasting common, "false" chemical practices with the true alchemical method, focusing on the movement of the "spirit" within the vessel and the arrival of the crucial "blackness" or nigredo stage.</meta>

There are those for whom it is taken from **eggshells** <note>original: "testis ovorum." Powdered eggshells were commonly used in early chemistry as a source of calcium carbonate.</note> and **quicklime** <term>quicklime</term> (original: "calce viva"): Calcium oxide, a caustic substance produced by heating limestone, often used by alchemists as a drying agent or to create heat.,
    And in its own way, it is made to perish.
Yet this **sublimation** <note>In alchemy, sublimation is the process of heating a substance until it vaporizes and then allowing it to condense back into a solid on a cooler surface, thereby purifying it.</note> is of no use at all,
    Since it is far removed from my purpose.
Let there be only this one process, therefore, which we intend:
    Take care of this one thing—that the **spirit** <term>spirit</term> (original: "spiritus"): The volatile, gaseous essence of the substance. does not seek the heights of the vessel. <note>The author warns that if the vapor stays too high or escapes, it will not properly interact with the "body" at the bottom of the flask.</note>
For without force, it is scarcely driven back down when pressed,
    Since it desires to reside in the highest place.
Scarcely does this elevated matter enjoy staying anywhere else,
    For it seeks the cooling <note>original: "refrigerium." Alchemical vessels often had a cooler "neck" where vapors would condense.</note> while it is heated by the fire.
Rather, keep it lower down until the **earth grows black**; <note>original: "terra nigrescat." This refers to the *Nigredo* or "Black Work," the first essential stage of the Great Work, symbolizing death, purification, and the breakdown of the old form.</note>
    And let the **Work** <term>Work</term> (original: "opus"): The *Magnum Opus* or Great Work—the entire process of creating the Philosopher's Stone. show signs of a dark color.

<summary>The author warns against using common materials like eggshells for sublimation, instead instructing the practitioner to keep the "spirit" low in the vessel until the matter reaches the critical "blackness" stage of purification.</summary>

    Having been made entirely **spiritual** <note>The term "spiritual" here refers to volatility—the ability of the substance to rise as a gas or vapor, leaving its heavy, material "body" behind.</note>, flies through the air:
Then let the heaven seek the earth again, and let the body
    Be bound to the **spirit** <term>spirit</term> (original: "spiritui"), so that the two joined together may become a **Monad** <term>Monad</term> (original: "Monas"): A single, indivisible unity; the ultimate philosophical goal where the volatile (spirit) and the fixed (body) are perfectly united..
And what was heaven, let it then become earth: which very thing
    Will happen, if it seeks the heights **seven times** <note>original: "septenâ vice." The number seven is sacred in alchemy, representing a complete cycle of transformation or the seven planetary influences.</note>.
For the body is carried up into the air for three reasons:
    First, so that it may become spiritual.
Second, so that the spirit might take up the fixed limbs of the body;
    And become one and the same with this body.

<unclear>it will be able</unclear>...

<summary>This page describes the purification of the alchemical Tria Prima (Sulfur and Salt) to reach the "whiter than snow" state of Albedo. It details the opening of the Eighth Gate and the process of joining the volatile spirit with the fixed body to create a unified Monad.</summary>
<keywords>Albedo, Eighth Gate, Sublimation, Sulfur, Salt, Spirit, Monad, Fermentation, Purification</keywords>

## IX
# ON FERMENTATION <note>original: "DE FERMENTATIONE"</note>

True **Fermentation** <term>Fermentation</term> (original: "Fermentatio"): In the alchemical tradition, this is the process of "seeding" the purified matter with a small amount of the Stone (the ferment) to give it a specific quality, much like yeast transforms dough. is known by very few;
Therefore, I shall reveal this secret in my verses.
I have tirelessly traveled through various regions, yet
Scarcely one was found who could have taught it.
These secrets finally lay open to me by the favor
Of the **Divinity** <note>original: "Numinis." It was common for alchemical authors to claim their knowledge came through divine revelation rather than just human study.</note>; therefore, receive them with attentive ears.
Indeed, **Ferments** are prepared in various ways,
By which the **Medicine of the Wise** <term>Medicine of the Wise</term> (original: "Medicina Sophi"): A term for the Philosopher's Stone, which was believed to be a universal medicine capable of curing both "sick" (base) metals and human ailments. flourishes and continues.
Some dissolve the **two lights** <note>original: "duo lumina." This refers to the Sun (Gold) and the Moon (Silver), the solar and lunar principles required for the Work.</note> into clear waters,
And join those things to be congealed to their own ash. <note>original: "cineri iungunt ista gelanda suo." This refers to the process of recombining the "spirit" (the dissolved liquid) with the "body" (the solid residue or ash) so that they may freeze or solidify together.</note>

<summary>This page introduces the ninth stage of the alchemical process, Fermentation, emphasizing its secrecy and the necessity of divine favor in understanding it. It describes the initial step of dissolving gold and silver into "waters" to be recombined with their solid remains.</summary>
<keywords>Fermentation, Medicine of the Wise, Two Lights, gold and silver, alchemical secrets, Ninth Gate</keywords>

--- Page 152 ---
<meta>This page continues the discussion of Fermentation from the previous page. The author distinguishes between "false" methods involving excessive heat or incorrect sublimation and the "truer" paths followed by those who imitate Nature more closely.</meta>

But if they undergo the trials of **Vulcan** <note>original: "uulcani." Vulcan was the Roman god of fire; in alchemy, this refers to the intense, destructive heat of a furnace.</note>, they are suddenly
Burned by the flames, and they cannot survive.
This kind of **Fermentation** <term>Fermentation</term> (original: "Fermentatio"): The process of "seeding" the alchemical matter to give it a specific quality or "life." is not our purpose,
Since this is not performed by the aid of **Nature**.
Certain people follow the footsteps of **Nature** more closely,
Fermenting their prepared medicines in this manner.
For they resolve the **Sun** and the **Moon** <note>original: "solem Lunamque." These are the standard alchemical symbols for Gold and Silver.</note> into **Mercury**,
Adding them to **spirits** <note>original: "spiritibus." Volatile substances that can be raised or "sublimated" by heat.</note> until they fly into the heights.
They carry these upward to the sublime <note>original: "In sublime." A reference to the process of sublimation, where solids are turned into vapor and re-solidify.</note> twice, thrice, and four times,
So that they may take the place of **Ferment** (which is a **false way** <note>original: "via falsa."</note>).
There are others, who think themselves more blessed,
Who prepare for themselves **Ferments** that are nearer to the truth.

<summary>The author warns against using violent fire (Vulcan) that destroys the work and critiques a common "false way" of fermentation involving the repeated sublimation of gold and silver. He begins to transition toward those who seek a more natural path.</summary>

<keywords>Vulcan, Fire, Fermentation, Nature, Sun and Moon, Gold and Silver, Mercury, Sublimation, False Way</keywords>

--- Page 153 ---
<meta>This page continues the discussion of those seekers who are closer to the truth but still lack the final key of putrefaction. It follows the previous page's description of various methods of preparing "Ferments."</meta>

These men, along with **Mercury** <term>Mercury</term> (original: "Mercurius"): Not just the liquid metal quicksilver, but the "Universal Solvent" in alchemy used to break down and purify solid substances., reduce their bodies to a paste,
And they cause the **Medicine** <term>Medicine</term> (original: "Medicina"): A common alchemical term for the Philosopher's Stone or the Elixir, which was believed to "heal" base metals into gold and restore human health. to fall upon them.
To these seekers, some secrets are partially revealed; but for them,
Because the end is not yet reached, individual truths are not fully open.
For they do not cause the **ferment** <term>Ferment</term> (original: "fermentum"): A substance added to the alchemical work to "leaven" the matter and give it the quality of gold or silver. to rot <note>original: "putrefacere." In the alchemical process, "putrefaction" or the "Black Work" (Nigredo) is a vital stage where the substance must "die" or decompose before it can be reborn in a perfected state.</note>, and likewise,
Since they change nothing, the **original form** <note>original: "pristina forma." The initial, impure state of the material.</note> remains.
For this reason, I bring such great gifts of the secret to you alone,
Moved by a true love for you.
Reflect on the care you gave to the raw body:
This same care will be required again for the perfected body.
Specifically, let these things first rot in the proper order,
So that, through rotting, the **original form** of the matter may perish.

<summary>The author explains that even those who use Mercury and the Medicine correctly often fail because they do not allow the material to "putrefy" or rot. He emphasizes that the original form of a substance must be destroyed through this process before it can be perfected.</summary>

<keywords>Mercury, Medicine, Ferment, Putrefaction, Original Form, Alchemy, Transformation, Nigredo, Perfection</keywords>

--- Page 154 ---
<meta>This page continues the technical instructions for the alchemical process. Having previously emphasized the necessity of "putrefaction" (the breakdown of the original form), the author now moves to the specific proportions and methods for preparing the "Ferment," which acts like a leaven to transform the base matter into gold or silver.</meta>

For you are commanded to transform the matter before you begin the fermentation;
This is the very core and head of my teaching.
Let only a fourth part of the **Ferment** <term>Ferment</term> (original: "fermentum"): A substance added to the alchemical work to "leaven" the matter, imparting the specific qualities of gold or silver. enter your compound—
The same ferment which **Sun** <note>original: "Sol." In alchemy, the Sun represents Gold.</note> or **Moon** <note>original: "Luna." In alchemy, the Moon represents Silver.</note> shall provide to you.
Therefore, come now, if you wish to be a **Master** <term>Master</term> (original: "Magister"): An adept or expert who has achieved the Great Work. in our art,
See that you prepare the ferment in this manner:
Fix the solid earth and the liquid <note>original: "limpha," literally "clear water" or "lymph."</note> together;
Let an **Amalgam** <term>Amalgam</term> (original: "Amalgama"): A soft or liquid alloy, usually created by mixing mercury with another metal. be ready
Into which you may cast the matter when the **Medicine** <term>Medicine</term> (original: "Medicina"): The alchemical Elixir or Philosopher's Stone, capable of "healing" base metals into gold. begins to flow.
Let these, once mixed together, be sealed within a single glass vessel,
So that the **fire** <note>original: "ignis." Alchemists used various "grades" of heat, often comparing the initial stage to the gentle warmth of a brooding hen.</note> may nourish them at a suitable temperature.
Continue in this way until the substances are sufficiently fixed and fermented,
Reaching a state that satisfies your soul.

<summary>The author provides the specific formula for the "Ferment," instructing the practitioner to mix one part ferment (derived from gold or silver) with four parts of the compound. The process involves creating an amalgam in a sealed glass vessel under carefully regulated heat until the substance is "fixed."</summary>
<keywords>Fermentation, Sol (Gold), Luna (Silver), Amalgam, Medicine, Alchemical Fire, Fixation</keywords>

--- Page 155 ---
<meta>Continuing the instructions for the Great Work, this page focuses on the "Projection" phase and the vital necessity of the "Ferment." Cast then a portion of the powder, however much you desire,

If you prepare the **ferments** <term>Ferments</term> (original: "fermenta"): Specific substances (usually pure gold or silver) added to the alchemical Stone to "direct" its transformative power. for yourself, a white color will be desired,

For just as flour, when worked into a mass of grain <note>original: "frumenti," referring to the dough or bulk of the bread.</note>,

That flavor which ought to be its natural state:

Which are less harmful to your perception <note>original: "gustu," literally "taste," but here referring to the quality or the refined nature of the work.</note>;
And which can endure the **fire** <note>original: "ignis." A central goal of alchemy was to create a "fixed" substance—one that remains stable and does not evaporate or burn away even in the most intense heat.</note>, and are not consumed by the fire;
But rather, they always seek their food from the flame.

<summary>The author uses a domestic metaphor of baking bread to explain that the alchemical Medicine requires a "ferment" to be effective and nourishing. This process ensures the resulting gold or silver is "fixed" and able to withstand heat without being destroyed.</summary>

There are two ferments of three kinds; provide the **bodies** <term>Bodies</term> (original: "corpora"): The metallic substances, usually gold or silver, that serve as the physical base for the transformation.

Is first called **Earth** <term>Earth</term> (original: "tellus"): The solid matter or precipitate at the bottom of the vessel. along with **Green Water** <term>Green Water</term> (original: "viridi aqua"): Often a reference to "vitriol" (sulfuric acid) or a specific acidic solvent used to break down metallic "bodies."

This refers to the stage of "putrefaction," where the chemical mixture breaks down and usually turns black, viewed by alchemists as a death before rebirth.</note> lie in slow-acting **manure** <term>Manure</term> (original: "fimo"): Heat was essential; alchemists often buried their glass vessels in warm horse dung to maintain a gentle, steady temperature for months.

As to where this matter flows from, and what it represents to the **Sages** <note>original: "Sophis," referring to the "Philosophers" or master alchemists.</note>.
Soon we must face the scrutiny of those who practice the art,
Though it is clear they grasp little or nothing of this.
For everywhere you may see many speaking of **Robert's bow and string** <note>original: "de neruo, arcuque Roberto." This is a Latin rendering of a common medieval proverb: "Many speak of Robin Hood who never shot his bow." Whose words carry no weight at all.

It no longer knows the path of a freer **will** <term>Will</term> (original: "arbitrij"): Used here metaphorically to mean "freedom of movement" or "volatility." Once the substance is "fixed" through fermentation, it loses its ability to evaporate or change uncontrollably.;

## X
# ON EXALTATION. <term>Exaltation</term> (original: "Exaltatio"): A process in alchemy where a substance's inner heat and "virtue" are increased, effectively raising its potency and dignity to a higher state.

Now the **Exaltation** comes to be celebrated by my Muses.
Let it be your care to know this perfectly,
From which our **sublimation** <term>sublimation</term> (original: "sublimatio"): The process of heating a substance until it turns into vapor and then solidifies again; here, it is described as nearly identical to exaltation. differs only slightly,
Provided that you correctly grasp my words which I have spoken:
To this process, the sacred teachings of the Divine Word agree,
Where **Christ** <note>original: "Cristus." The author frequently uses the "Christ-as-Lapis" (Christ as the Stone) analogy, suggesting that the purification of matter mirrors the resurrection or ascension of Christ.</note> utters such sounds from his mouth:
"Behold, if I shall have been **exalted** into the upper airs,
Then I will draw all things to myself with a **life-giving breath**." <note>original: "flatú vivificante." This is a paraphrase of the Gospel of John 12:32. In an alchemical context, it refers to the "spirit" of the stone attracting and transforming lower metals.</note>
Thus, if our **Medicine** <note>original: "Medicina." The finalized alchemical elixir or Philosopher's Stone, capable of curing both metals and human bodies.</note> is now raised into the airs,
It will excel, ennobled by its own gifts.

<summary>This page defines the alchemical stage of Exaltation, drawing a direct parallel between the elevation of the "Medicine" and the biblical exaltation of Christ. ## XI
# ON MULTIPLICATION.

Now let it be defined for the **Sages** <note>original: "Sophis." Refers to the "Wise" or the initiated practitioners of alchemy.</note> what **Multiplication** <term>Multiplication</term> (original: "Multiplicatio"): The stage in the alchemical process where the quantity and the potency of the Philosopher's Stone are increased through repeated cycles of heating. is;
Muse, recount it, for I believe you know every detail.
It is the increase of the **red**, and at the same time of the **white Elixir**, <note>In alchemy, the "White Elixir" transmutes metals into silver, while the "Red Elixir"—the more potent form—transmutes them into gold.</note>
Which excels in weight and is powerful in quality.
It is called the increase of the **Medicine**, <note>original: "Medicinae." Another name for the Philosopher's Stone, emphasizing its power to "heal" or perfect imperfect matter.</note> in weight, in scent,
In virtue, and in any fire, heat, or degree.
So that you may be able to extend your Elixir without end,
And multiply its strengths for your own benefit.
The underlying cause is the **fire**; <note>original: "ignis." This refers to the "secret" or "philosophical" fire that drives the transformation within the vessel.</note> which is never extinguished,
But once kindled, it acts in such a way that it shall never die.

<note>Vocabulary list from the original Latin: **Multiplication** (Multiplicatio), **Sages** (Sophis), **Elixir**, **Medicine** (Medicina), **fire** (ignis), **alchemy** (alchemia).</note>

<summary>This page defines the eleventh stage of the alchemical Great Work, Multiplication, explaining how the Philosopher's Stone is increased in quantity and potency. It emphasizes the role of a perpetual, inextinguishable fire in extending the "Medicine's" power indefinitely.</summary>
<keywords>Multiplication, Red Elixir, White Elixir, Philosophical Fire, The Medicine, Alchemy, Sages</keywords>

# ON PROJECTION
<note>original: "De Proiectione." In alchemy, "projection" is the final, transformative step where a small amount of the Philosopher’s Stone (the "tincture") is cast upon a base metal to transmute it into gold or silver.</note>

-> Projection confirms the Practical Work by its own utility, <-
-> of which I hand down certain mystical secrets to you. <-
-> If the tincture <note>original: "tinctura"; the concentrated essence or "Stone" capable of staining or "tinging" base metals into noble ones.</note> is not to have its color changed, <-
-> and to see if it is constant, you may test it by this method: <-
-> Mix a small portion of it with metal, <-
-> or cast it into fuming mercury; <note>original: "Mercurium"; mercury was often used as the "base" for projection due to its fluid, metallic nature.</note> <-
-> Soon, adhering steadily like liquid pitch, <-
-> it will tinge the mercury for you through projection. <-
-> It will enter and spread itself widely enough <-
-> that it may endure the flames of the fire. <note>The ultimate proof of a successful alchemical medicine was its "fixity"—its ability to remain stable and not evaporate or perish when subjected to intense heat.</note> <-

Often, however, mistakes are made here through error, and the work
is destroyed; for many, the labor is without fruit.
For if the Medicine <note>original: "Medicina"; in alchemy, the "Medicine" refers to the Elixir or the Philosopher’s Stone, which was thought to "heal" or perfect base metals into gold.</note> is applied upon impure metal,

that brittle substance upon pure bodies.

<meta>The previous page concluded with the instruction to multiply by ten, explaining that ten times ten equals one hundred. This page continues that mathematical progression, illustrating the exponential "Multiplication" of the Stone's power.</meta>

If you multiply a hundred by ten in its turn,
there will be a thousand for you from the figures, if they are counted.
If a thousand is again led back through ten for you,
ten thousand, if you count them, are numbered.
And if you multiply these by ten,
from those numbers you possess a hundred thousand once more.
If thousands are multiplied so many times by those same
thousands, there will scarcely be a number that can be counted.
In this way, you can increase the amount, which grows so abundantly
that you can hardly calculate the numbers with numbers.
Since I reveal these secrets <note>original: "secreta"; in an alchemical context, this refers to the "hidden" knowledge of the "Multiplication" of the Stone’s power.</note> to you, my Son <note>original: "Fili"; a traditional term of affection used by an alchemical master for his student.</note>,
may you in return offer the prayers that are owed on my behalf.

<note>This text is a mathematical poem written in elegiac couplets <note>A traditional form of Latin poetry consisting of alternating lines of dactylic hexameter and pentameter.</note>, instructing the reader on the powers of ten. It describes how multiplying 100 by 10 yields 1,000; multiplying 1,000 by 10 yields 10,000; multiplying that by 10 yields 100,000; and finally, multiplying large numbers by themselves creates a sum nearly beyond counting. In the context of the "Great Work," this refers to the stage of Multiplication, where the Philosopher's Stone is said to increase in both quantity and quality each time the process is repeated.</note>

## ->THE END OF THE TWELVE GATES<-

<note>This concluding section marks the completion of the "Twelve Gates," a famous alchemical poem by George Ripley. Having passed through the metaphorical gates (stages such as Calcination, Dissolution, etc.), the student is now considered a master of the "Fortress" of the Stone. The author emphasizes the ethical dimension of alchemy—that this knowledge must be kept from those who would use it for evil—and the practical necessity of "Multiplication," where the Stone is repeatedly refined in the fire to increase its potency.</note>

<summary>This page concludes George Ripley’s Twelve Gates, celebrating the student's mastery over the alchemical process and emphasizing the need for secrecy and the continued multiplication of the Tincture's power.</summary>
<keywords>George Ripley, Twelve Gates, Tincture, Multiplication, Alchemy, Secrecy, Philosophical Stone</keywords>

# RECAPITULATION

I will arrange all I have said into a system,
And conclude in my brief verse those things already recounted.

Pay heed, therefore, with a watchful mind to the figure,
Which contains secrets both great and small.

If you begin at the same time to join Practice <term>Practice</term> <gloss>original: "Praxin"; the physical laboratory work, as opposed to "Theoria" or contemplation.</gloss> to what you have read—
As the writings, the figure, and the color will show you—

It cannot happen that you lose the profit of your labors,
Or plow the dry shore with a barren plowshare <note>original: "sterili... vomere littus ares"; a classical metaphor (derived from Ovid or Virgil) meaning to engage in futile, wasted effort. The author promises that if the student follows the "Practice" correctly, their work will be fertile rather than wasted.</note>.

At the start, turn over in your wise mind,
How broad this stone is, and how vast it is.

Begin beneath the first side marked by learned art,
Where Phoebus <note>original: "Phoebus"; the Greco-Roman sun god, used here to represent the "Solar" principle or the Golden quality of the material.</note> plunges his weary horses into the abyss <note>This imagery refers to the sunset or the West. In alchemical symbolism, the "setting of the sun" often signifies the beginning of the first stage of the work, the Nigredo (blackening), where the material is "killed" or dissolved into darkness before being reborn.</note>.

Then with Mercury, which is made most pure by fire,

# WARNING TO THE READER

Now I shall recount, O reader, the labors of the Sun and Moon <term>Phoebus and Luna: Poetic names for the Sun and Moon, representing the metals Gold and Silver.</term>
->Which I have endured, so that you might be made cautious.<-
The work of cinnabar <term>Cinnabar: A bright red mineral (mercury sulfide) once considered a primary ingredient for the Stone, but often dismissed as a "false" path by later masters.</term> provides nothing; nor does mercury, when carried on high, <note>original: "vectus in altum"; a reference to the process of sublimation where a substance is heated until it rises as vapor.</note>
->Benefit you at all; therefore, beware.<-
For me, the sublimation of spirits <term>Spirits: Volatile chemical substances—such as sulfur, arsenic, or sal ammoniac—that "flee" the fire as vapor.</term> was performed in vain;
->Ferments offer no help; the use of salt holds no value.<-
The labor of steel and iron will never grant you anything,
->Believe me, my stone <term>The Stone: The Philosopher's Stone, the legendary catalyst capable of transmuting base metals into gold.</term> shall not be made from these.<-
Indeed, I myself prepared every work according to the books,
->But all that labor was without its fruit.<-

<vocab>Sun (Gold), Moon (Silver), Cinnabar, Mercury, Spirits, Sublimation, Ferments, Salt, Steel, The Stone</vocab>

<summary>A cautionary poem in which the author warns the reader against common but fruitless alchemical experiments involving salts, iron, and the sublimation of mercury. He emphasizes that following textbook recipes literally led him only to failure, setting the stage for a more "true" revelation of the art.</summary>
<keywords>Admonition, alchemical failure, cinnabar, mercury sublimation, Phoebus and Luna, salts in alchemy, metals, the Stone, alchemical poetry</keywords>

<meta>This page continues the "Warning to the Reader" from the previous folio. I have encountered books that are false and riddled with errors;
->Because of them, after all my labors, I remained sorrowful and like an exile. <note>original: "exsul"; likely a metaphor for wandering lost in error or being "exiled" from the truth of the Great Work.</note><-
Corrosive substances cause harm; abandon those "burning waters," <term>Burning waters: Corrosive mineral acids, such as nitric acid (aqua fortis), which were often used to dissolve metals but were frequently deemed "deadly" or "false" paths by alchemists seeking the living Stone.</term>
->Even when I prepared these things, I reaped no benefit.<-
Quite often I refined the ashes of eggshells <term>Calces of eggs: Calcinated or powdered eggshells, a source of calcium carbonate often used in the "minor" works of alchemy.</term> two or three times over,
->Attempting to draw forth an oil from the dry remains.<-
From this, a separation of the elements <term>Division of elements: The fundamental alchemical process of breaking a substance down into its constituent parts—Earth, Air, Fire, and Water—to purify them.</term> was frequently performed;
->Yet, I found none of the profits I sought.<-
Fools have called Vitriol <term>Vitriol: A metallic sulfate salt. In its "green" form (iron sulfate), it was a common starting material for chemistry but often a distraction for the novice.</term> the "Green Lion," <term>Green Lion: A multi-layered symbol in alchemy. While it can represent the "solvent" that dissolves gold, the author warns here against the literal use of common green vitriol.</term>
->Because of this, a vain hope promised me gold-bearing riches.<-
Arsenic, orpiment <note>original: "Solis pigmentum"; literally "the pigment of the Sun," a common name for yellow orpiment (arsenic trisulfide).</note>, Sulfur, and everything else:
->That which has a weak foundation will inevitably collapse of its own accord.<-

A weak beginning ends without any gain. <note>original: "Debile principium sine lucro desinit esse."</note>
Error was my beginning, and fraud was my end.
My stomach was never healthy, my clothing was always filthy,
And my coins were nothing but smoke. <note>This vividly describes the "puffer," a derogatory term for an amateur alchemist who ruined their health and wealth by literally following recipes in front of a bellows-stoked furnace.</note>
Alkali salt, rock salt, <term>Rock salt: Referred to in Latin as "sal gemmae," meaning salt that is clear or crystalline like a gem.</term> and indeed everything that can ever
Go by the name of "salt"—imagine them all, I pray.
From here let the stench of quicksilver <term>Quicksilver: A common name for mercury (argentum vivum), prized for its liquid metallic state but dangerous to work with.</term> depart,
And let Mercury itself be cast far away.
Command the imperfect metals <term>Imperfect metals: Base metals such as lead, tin, or copper, which alchemists believed were "diseased" versions of gold or silver.</term> to stay far away;
Believe me, it is in vain that our Work will "redden" <note>The "reddening" or *rubedo* is the final, highest stage of the Great Work, signifying the successful creation of the Philosopher's Stone.</note> by using these.
I have tried everything: hair, salts, eggs, blood,
And marcasites: <term>Marcasites: In early chemistry, this term was applied broadly to various metallic ores (like iron pyrites) that shimmered with a metallic luster but yielded no precious metal.</term> yet I gained nothing from them.

# Physical Description: The Fore-edge

->**View of the Bound Volume**<-

The **fore-edge** <term>fore-edge: the edge of a book's leaves opposite the spine</term> of this thick manuscript is shown in a vertical view. The individual edges of the many paper leaves are visible, having aged to a brownish-tan color. They are decorated with a faint marbling consisting of red and green speckles, a common decorative technique for high-quality bindings.

The binding itself is made of light-colored **vellum** <term>vellum: a durable binding material made from animal skin, typically calfskin</term>, which is seen on either side of the stacked pages. At the bottom of the image, one can see the structural sewing of the **quires** <term>quires: the gathered and folded sections of paper that are sewn together to form the book</term>.

<note>This image is overlaid with a faint digital watermark from the Vatican Apostolic Library (Bibliotheca Apostolica Vaticana).</note>

# Physical Characteristics: The Fore-edge

->**View of the Closed Volume**<-

This photograph captures the **fore-edge** <term>fore-edge: the edge of a book's leaves opposite the spine</term> of the thick manuscript. Because this is an image of the closed book, there is no legible text to transcribe from the internal pages.

The binding, visible at the outer edges of the stack, is made of cream-colored **vellum** <term>vellum: a durable binding material made from treated animal skin</term>. The numerous paper leaves are pressed tightly together, and their edges have been decorated with a red-speckled **marbling** <term>marbling: a decorative pattern of ink or paint applied to the edges of a book to prevent staining and for aesthetic appeal</term>.

In the center of the leaf edges, one can see very faint, large handwritten characters. These likely represent an old shelfmark <note>A library's unique identification code or volume number, often written on the edge so the book could be identified while stored on a shelf.</note>, though the ink has faded and worn away too much to be deciphered.

## The Splendor of the Work

->When you have taken up the honor of such great splendor from these things,<-
->The earth left behind will be of no use to you.<-
->I call the whole **composition** by the name of our **Lead**;<-
<term>composition: the refined alchemical mixture; Lead: not common lead, but the "Lead of the Wise," a foundational substance in the Great Work.</term>
->I say; the common kind lacks fertility.<-
<note>The author distinguishes between "vulgatum" (common) metals and the "philosophical" versions used in alchemy.</note>
->That which shines with a red color comes from the light of the **Sun**;<-
<note>original: "Solis"; in alchemical symbolism, the Sun represents Gold and the masculine principle.</note>
->But from the **Moon** comes splendor and white honor.<-
<note>original: "Luna"; represents Silver and the feminine principle.</note>
->Finally, I shall bind many things under a single embrace:<-
->These three I truly call our **menstruums**.<-
<term>menstruums: from the Latin "menstrua," these are alchemical solvents or catalytic liquids used to dissolve "bodies" and facilitate transformation.</term>
->If we truly reduce perfect **bodies** by our art<-
<term>bodies: the physical forms of metals, such as gold and silver, which must be broken down.</term>
->Into **calx**, that will not be a labor of the hands.<-
<note>original: "calcem"; a fine powder produced by heating a substance. The author suggests this reduction happens through chemical "art" rather than physical grinding.</note>
->Only the **Green Lion** boasts purity within itself;<-
<term>Green Lion: a famous alchemical symbol, often representing strong acids (like nitric acid or "vitriol") or the raw, mineral state of mercury that "devours" the sun (gold).</term>
->All other things are completely filled with their own defects.<-

<summary>This page explains the symbolic "Lead" of the alchemists and the necessity of reducing metals into a powder (calx) using three specific solvents. It introduces the "Green Lion" as the only pure substance capable of perfecting the work.</summary>
<keywords>alchemy, Lead of the Philosophers, Sun and Moon, menstruum, Green Lion, calx, transmutation</keywords>
